expect(missing).toEqual([]);\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'finds each authored wording inside its rule, and the two conditions never overlap\', () => {\\r\\n  for (const [statement, [sufficient, necessary]] of spanEntries()) {\\r\\n    const found = [sufficient, necessary].map(entry => entry.split(\'|\').map(text => locateWording(statement, text.trim())));\\r\\n    for (const group of found) for (const place of group) expect(place, statement).not.toBeNull();\\r\\n    const [s, n] = found.map(group => group[0]!);\\r\\n    expect(s[1] < n[0] || n[1] < s[0], statement).toBe(true);\\r\\n    expect(roleOfSpan(statement, s), statement).toBe(\'sufficient\');\\r\\n    expect(roleOfSpan(statement, n), statement).toBe(\'necessary\');\\r\\n  }\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nit(\'accepts either order, a dropped article, and rejects a span that swallows the indicator\', () => {\\r\\n  const rule = \'A warranty is valid if the product is registered within 30 days.\';\\r\\n  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[0, 3], [5, 11]]).correct).toBe(true);\\r\\n  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[1, 3], [4, 11]]).correct).toBe(false);\\r\\n  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[5, 11], [1, 3]]).correct).toBe(true);\\r\\n  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[0, 3], [4, 11]]).correct).toBe(false);\\r\\n  expect(checkConditionSpans(rule, [[0, 3]]).correct).toBe(false);\\r\\n  expect(spanText(rule, [5, 11])).toBe(\'the product is registered within 30 days\');\\r\\n});\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"9d344e","wall_time_seconds":0.6947741,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3907,"output":"### Integrate complete source banks through a progressive course, 2026-10-11\\r\\nStatus: explicit implementation authorization, not acceptance or publication.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua wants the finished course, not another isolated prototype or plan. Create the conceptual contrapositive note first, compare the actual Intro to LR lesson/drill progression, then integrate every source question at the point its prerequisites have been taught. Preserve the existing 24 foundation questions; rewriting them is not required. Keep familiar statements when the learner performs a new operation. Preserve all original source text and record corrections separately. Improve interaction and visual design, not merely copy worksheets into cards. Show the complete sequence at the end.\\r\\n\\r\\nImplementation direction: concept and example, learner decision, explanation; then indicator selection, complete-condition placement, forward diagrams, faithful if/then rewriting, formal contraposition, and full mixed practice. Keep prose self-review distinct from reliable automatic checks, local-device persistence distinct from account records, and source coverage distinct from mastery. Entire-bank availability does not require one long sitting. Provide short-session resume and review.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2026-10-11: source procedure matters as much as coverage\\r\\n\\r\\n- Joshua rejected the legacy sufficient-practice drill after source-bank integration. Counting imported items does not prove that the source teaching procedure was implemented. Audit every existing drill route alongside new source sets.\\r\\n- Preserve the notes\' progression: indicators, complete conditions, learner-defined symbols, original arrow, contrapositive, English readbacks. Introduce each prerequisite before testing it.\\r\\n- Fade assistance across the course. Guided recognition can supply choices; independent translation must begin with blank production and withhold models until the whole attempt is written.\\r\\n- Keep one active operation, the source rule in view, readable stage progress and specific feedback. Correct objectively wrong answers before advancing; label written comparison honestly as self-review.\\r\\n- Validate actual rendered contrast, responsive layout, stage gating and draft recovery. A source import, passing tests or a wrapper stylesheet alone is not a polished drill.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n## Gold standard: the Contrapositive lesson page (approved 2026-10-12)\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua called the finished page \\"perfect\\". Treat it as the template for every other concept lecture: same order, same density, same rules. Do not copy its wording or its examples. Rework the structure for the new topic and pick fresh examples.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Where it lives:** `ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/` (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx` `ContrapositiveNote` and `FourRules`, `ContrapositiveVisuals.tsx`, `contrapositive-visuals.css`), commit `383747e9` plus the uncommitted follow-ups of 2026-10-12. Video project: `ai-os-projects/contrapositive-video/lecture` (`script.json`, `index.html`, `build_audio.py`). Full correction log: `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Page shape (about 600 words of text, seven sections)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **What it is.** Plain definition first, in a navy panel, then one short example. No run-up.\\r\\n2. **Why it matters or what it gives you.** Two or three sentences. The key term pulses, one exclamation at most.\\r\\n3. **Watch the lesson.** A 60 to 90 second narrated video placed after the idea is introduced. Captions track, poster image, written fallback that only renders when opened or when the video fails.\\r\\n4. **How to write it.** Two bold steps (Step 1 and Step 2), then an interactive demo with \\"Step 1. Click here to ...\\" and \\"Step 2. Click here to ...\\" buttons. A second demo uses a fresh, simple, intuitive example where one side already starts negated, so the rule that two nots cancel is shown, not stated. Each demo shows the rule as an if-then sentence above the diagram and the result as an if-then sentence below.\\r\\n5. **What it is not.** One line, then slim cards: red \\"Wrong\\" for each look-alike, green \\"Right\\" for the answer. Badge, one-line change, diagram. Nothing else.\\r\\n6. **A short habit paragraph.** One paragraph, no list.\\r\\n7. **Pop Quiz.** Centered title with a letter-pop and light-sweep effect. Four items, one at a time. Ideas shown as short phrases, never letters. Right answer explanation is one sentence (the answer as a natural if-then). Wrong answer explanation is \\"Not that one.\\" plus one specific reason about that choice. No intro paragraph, no category tag lines.\\r\\n\\r\\n### What Joshua wanted (keep)\\r\\n\\r\\n- One main example carried through the page, plus one fresh second example. Never the same example in more places than that, and never an example already used on another page.\\r\\n- Natural if-then sentences for every rule and result. Diagrams support the sentence; they never replace it.\\r\\n- Bold numbered steps. Controls say what to click. The student drives; nothing autoplays.\\r\\n- Colour carries meaning and travels with the idea (amber sufficient, teal necessary). Wrong looks wrong (red, X); right looks right (green, check).\\r\\n- Sleek, high-end visuals. A new effect for each new moment; never reuse the same trick. Every animation has a still equivalent and stops under reduced motion.\\r\\n- Short. When a page feels like too much, cut sections, not words: remove whole parts that repeat what the video, a demo or the quiz already does.\\r\\n- Ideas in quiz diagrams are condensed statements (\\"On the list\\", \\"Wristband\\"), not letter codes.\\r\\n- Narrated video in his voice standard, AI voice acceptable, built from the same wording as the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n### What Joshua rejected (never repeat)\\r\\n\\r\\n- A squiggle (~) for negation anywhere. Use the word \\"not\\" or a diagonal strike from the idea\'s top left to bottom right.\\r\\n- The raffle, coat, coffee, breathing, elevator, door and any example already used elsewhere. \\"Door\\", \\"patterns\\" and \\"move\\" as student words.\\r\\n- Sophistry and obligation metaphors (\\"promise\\", \\"depends on\\", \\"nothing to stand on\\"). Stock encouragement and run-ups (\\"Here\'s the good news\\", \\"Every time:\\").\\r\\n- Content repeated on the page, a legend that restates what a pill already says, a paragraph before the quiz explaining the quiz, tag lines that add nothing.\\r\\n- Complicated interactives: the first light-bulb stage (wires, chips, extra buttons) and the \\"four worlds\\" picker were both cut as too busy. One idea, one control, one sentence.\\r\\n- Long answer explanations. Explanations that talk about the diagram (\\"both ideas get a not\\") instead of saying the answer in plain English.\\r\\n- Letters standing in for ideas.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Process that produced it (work backwards from this)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Write the section list above for the new topic before any copy. Cut anything the video, a demo or the quiz already covers.\\r\\n2. Draft prose in Joshua\'s voice, then run the `humanizer` skill over it and the corrections log. No em dashes.\\r\\n3. Build the visuals one at a time, check each on the live preview (not only in tests), and ask what is new about each effect.\\r\\n4. Pin the page order and the key phrases in the content test; update the test when a section is deliberately cut.\\r\\n5. Video: one script file, narration synthesized per sentence, word timings for sync, scenes built in the page\'s own palette, rendered and checked frame by frame. Voice: Kokoro af_heart (author grade A) when no paid voice key exists.\\r\\n6. Log every new correction in `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` and here before moving on.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Necessary definition and careful door metaphor, 2026-10-13\\r\\nExplicit direction: Joshua\'s wording \\"The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens.\\" leads the \\"Where the certainty is\\" section of the Necessary page. The door metaphor from his source may be used carefully (sufficient opens the door; necessary is guaranteed on the other side). This narrows the earlier blanket rejection of \\"door\\" as a student word; \\"door\\" as a unit of content stays banned. Avoid copy that sounds AI-written. Acceptance: read the section aloud; the metaphor must not suggest cause, timing, or that the necessary condition is only true behind the door.\\r\\n---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: LSAT teaching voice standard\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-03\\r\\naliases: [LSAT voice reference, Conditional transcription writing standard]\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, writing]\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  - ../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT teaching: make the reasoning sound like someone thinking\\r\\n\\r\\n**This is Joshua\'s selected reference for writing LSAT teaching material.** Use it for bootcamp lessons, lecture notes, explanations, guided examples, drills, flashcards, quizzes, reference pages, and instructional feedback. It develops the teaching-content register in [the canonical teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md). That document still governs correspondence and marketing in their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected the supplied **Conditional Reasoning** transcription on 2026-10-02 because of its writing, tone, word choice, cadence, point of view, progression, and depth. The goal is to reproduce those qualities closely through original wording and examples. The central quality is patient, conversational reasoning that the reader can follow and eventually perform.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is a calibration example. This guide separates **observed features** from **instructions for our material**. A source habit is not automatically a rule, a research finding, or permission to import a topic. Matching the voice does not require repeating the same metaphors, anecdotes, exercise sentences, or personal claims.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Find the part you need\\r\\n\\r\\n- [The evidence and coverage](#the-evidence-and-coverage)\\r\\n- [The voice in one original passage](#the-voice-in-one-original-passage)\\r\\n- [How the teacher relates to the reader](#how-the-teacher-relates-to-the-reader)\\r\\n- [Sentence length and spoken cadence](#sentence-length-and-spoken-cadence)\\r\\n- [Word choice and useful verbal habits](#word-choice-and-useful-verbal-habits)\\r\\n- [Questions that make the reader think](#questions-that-make-the-reader-think)\\r\\n- [Transitions, repetition, metaphor, and emphasis](#transitions-explain-why-the-next-move-follows)\\r\\n- [The complete source progression and depth](#the-complete-source-progression-and-depth)\\r\\n- [What to carry into the Conditional Bootcamp](#what-to-carry-into-the-conditional-bootcamp)\\r\\n- [Accuracy and method boundaries](#accuracy-and-method-boundaries)\\r\\n- [Calibration examples and review](#calibration-examples-for-different-lsat-surfaces)\\r\\n- [A reusable writing brief](#a-reusable-writing-brief)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The evidence and coverage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [source summary](../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) identifies the [preserved HTML](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html), [provenance](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/PROVENANCE.md), and SHA-256. Every supplied section, FAQ entry, table, worked diagram, and drill item was read. HTML line references below refer to that unchanged archived file.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe document has six main sections and six subordinate headings. It ends with six basic drill items and an answer-key reference. One line is explicitly marked partially obscured. The answer key is not supplied. This analysis covers the entire attachment; it cannot reconstruct the missing line or establish the contents of the larger chapter.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe HTML says its formatting was adjusted. We can examine what a pause, table, side note, or separated example does for learning. We cannot claim its font, spacing, color, or CSS exactly reproduces the original publication. No author is identified in the file. The exercise wording attributed to Shakespeare is separate from the narrator\'s teaching voice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The voice in one original passage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following is a new calibration passage. Its example was created for this guide; it is not a source quotation or an approved bootcamp page.\\r\\n\\r\\n> A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Suppose a shop has this rule: **Every parcel with a blue sticker has been inspected.**\\r\\n>\\r\\n> You pick up a parcel with a blue sticker. What do you know about it?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> It has been inspected. The rule covers every parcel with that sticker, including the one in your hand. A blue-sticker parcel that hadn\'t been inspected would break the rule.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Now turn the parcel over. It has an inspection stamp, but you can\'t see a blue sticker. Does it have to have one?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> We don\'t know. The rule tells us something about parcels with blue stickers. It doesn\'t tell us that every inspected parcel gets a sticker.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> That\'s the distinction we need: **what the sentence makes certain, and what it leaves open.**\\r\\n\\r\\nNotice the opening definition before the example, the small prediction, the explanation connecting the rule to the case, and the change in what the reader is given. The last sentence names a distinction the reader has just seen. The passage has a conversational rhythm because it follows a person\'s thoughts. Casual vocabulary alone would not accomplish that.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn an interactive lesson, pause after the first question and again after the inspection-stamp question. Reveal each explanation after the learner has had an opportunity to answer. The printed passage shows the whole progression for calibration.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How the teacher relates to the reader\\r\\n\\r\\n### The reader is beside the teacher\\r\\n\\r\\nThe narrator talks directly to someone doing the work. “Let\'s,” “you,” and “we” keep the reader involved. The writing seldom steps back to describe what students generally do. It asks this reader to notice, try, decide, or check something now.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe reader is treated as capable of understanding ordinary meaning. The teacher supplies support when a technical step is new. In the opening, the source first gives the literal relationship, then asks the reader to take its certainty seriously. Later, it encourages a guess before revealing the contrapositive. These are expectations of participation, not claims that everything should feel easy.\\r\\n\\r\\n**For our writing:** speak to the reader without making their intelligence the subject. Do not copy a line such as “Because you\'re a thinking human.” Give them a manageable question and the information to answer it. If they miss it, explain the precise move that went wrong.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Confidence lives in the reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is decisive about a valid consequence. It also says “We don\'t know” when the information cannot settle a question. These two kinds of confidence belong together. Clear teaching can be certain about the limits of what is known.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe teacher does not apologize for asking the reader to think. The explanation makes the request reasonable. In the FAQ, the narrator acknowledges early mistakes and explains the value of practicing anyway. The tone combines patience with a specific demand: attempt the question, write the diagram, inspect the error.\\r\\n\\r\\n**For our writing:** state the consequence plainly and show why. Preserve legitimate uncertainty. Avoid changing a logical “could” into “must” to make the prose sound stronger. Avoid guarantees about learning outcomes, timing, or score changes unless we have evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Warmth points at the work\\r\\n\\r\\nEncouragement is usually tied to an action. The reader can look back at the lesson, make a provisional attempt, learn from a wrong translation, or build confidence by checking work. The warmth comes from useful help and a teacher who expects progress.\\r\\n"}'}]

Keep the checks and their timing together.\\",\\n2632:        \\"originalStatement\\": \\"Any exposure to extreme temperatures compromises sample integrity.\\",\\n2633:        \\"statement\\": \\"Any exposure to extreme temperatures compromises sample integrity.\\",\\n2641:        \\"necessary\\": \\"that sample\'s integrity is compromised\\",\\n2642:        \\"diagram\\": \\"a sample is exposed to extreme temperatures → that sample\'s integrity is compromised\\",\\n2643:        \\"contrapositive\\": \\"NOT (that sample\'s integrity is compromised) → NOT (a sample is exposed to extreme temperatures)\\",\\n2644:        \\"ifThen\\": \\"If a sample is exposed to extreme temperatures, then that sample\'s integrity is compromised.\\",\\n2645:        \\"contrapositiveWords\\": \\"If that sample\'s integrity is not compromised, then that sample is not exposed to extreme temperatures.\\",\\n2646:        \\"explanation\\": \\"The rule covers any such exposure. Study is sufficient within this premise.\\",\\n2895:        \\"explanation\\": \\"Keep the location consistent. The supplied rule does not make fun proof that beer is present.\\",\\n2947:        \\"explanation\\": \\"Read the words literally for this supplied rule. Pouring is the required result.\\",\\n3235:        \\"explanation\\": \\"Only points to the necessary category, fish. Apply the supplied rule even though other animals swim in real life.\\",\\n3316:        \\"explanation\\": \\"Dying young is sufficient for goodness within the supplied rule, not the reverse.\\",\\n3688:    shortTitle: \'Intro training: contrapositives\',\\n"}'}]

Last, write both in ordinary if/then language. Reverse the conditions and negate each complete idea, including its restrictions.\',\\r\\n  };\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/** A run of adjacent words the learner selected, with the group they chose (\'1\', \'2\' or \'\'). */\\r\\nexport interface IndicatorSelection { text: string; group: string }\\r\\nexport interface IndicatorCheck { correct: boolean; hint: string }\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const normalizeIndicator = (text: string) => text.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]/g, \'\').replace(/\\\\s+/g, \' \').trim();\\r\\n\\r\\n// Accepted alternatives. Items keep one keyed indicator; these rules decide which other selections mean the same thing.\\r\\nconst DETERMINER_TAILS = [\'those\', \'the ones\', \'these\'];\\r\\nconst QUANTIFIERS = [\'any\', \'every\', \'all\', \'each\', \'no\', \'anyone\', \'anything\', \'everyone\', \'everything\', \'none\'];\\r\\nconst WORD_ALTERNATIVES: Record<string, string[]> = { every: [\'each and every\'] };\\r\\nconst WHO_PHRASE = /^([a-z]+ )?who$/;\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction acceptsSelection(expected: string, text: string) {\\r\\n  if (text === expected || WORD_ALTERNATIVES[expected]?.includes(text)) return true;\\r\\n  for (const tail of DETERMINER_TAILS) {\\r\\n    // \\"Only those\\" was taught as \\"only\\": accept the head word, and the phrase continued by \\"who\\".\\r\\n    if (expected.endsWith(` ${tail}`) && (text === expected.slice(0, -tail.length - 1) || text === `${expected} who`)) return true;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  // A condition introduced by who: accept \\"who\\" or \\"[group] who\\" (e.g. \\"members who\\").\\r\\n  if ((expected === \'who\' || expected.endsWith(\' who\')) && WHO_PHRASE.test(text)) return true;\\r\\n  // \\"Every applicant who\\" selected as one run still marks the quantifier.\\r\\n  const words = text.split(\' \');\\r\\n  return QUANTIFIERS.includes(expected) && words.length <= 4 && words[0] === expected && words[words.length - 1] === \'who\';\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst containsRun = (outer: string, inner: string) => ` ${outer} `.includes(` ${inner} `);\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Grades the indicator step and returns a hint that never names the keyed answer. */\\r\\nexport function checkIndicatorSelection(item: Pick<SourceWorksheetItem, \'statement\' | \'indicators\'>, selections: IndicatorSelection[]): IndicatorCheck {\\r\\n  const expected = item.indicators.map(i => ({ text: normalizeIndicator(i.text), group: String(i.group) }));\\r\\n  const remaining = [...expected];\\r\\n  const statementWords = normalizeIndicator(item.statement).split(\' \');\\r\\n  const whoIntroducesCondition = statementWords.includes(\'who\') && expected.some(e => e.group === \'1\' && QUANTIFIERS.includes(e.text));\\r\\n  const extra: string[] = [];\\r\\n  const wrongGroup: string[] = [];\\r\\n  for (const selection of selections) {\\r\\n    const text = normalizeIndicator(selection.text);\\r\\n    const index = remaining.findIndex(e => acceptsSelection(e.text, text));\\r\\n    if (index >= 0) {\\r\\n      if (remaining[index].group !== selection.group) wrongGroup.push(selection.text);\\r\\n      remaining.splice(index, 1);\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    // Two adjacent indicators (\\"must always\\") form one run of selected words.\\r\\n    const pairs: [number, number][] = [];\\r\\n    for (let i = 0; i < remaining.length; i++) for (let j = 0; j < remaining.length; j++) if (i !== j && `${remaining[i].text} ${remaining[j].text}` === text) pairs.push([i, j]);\\r\\n    const pair = pairs[0];\\r\\n    if (pair) {\\r\\n      if (remaining[pair[0]].group !== selection.group || remaining[pair[1]].group !== selection.group) wrongGroup.push(selection.text);\\r\\n      for (const i of [...pair].sort((a, b) => b - a)) remaining.splice(i, 1);\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    if (whoIntroducesCondition && WHO_PHRASE.test(text)) {\\r\\n      if (selection.group !== \'1\') wrongGroup.push(selection.text);\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    extra.push(selection.text);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (extra.length) {\\r\\n    const text = extra[0];\\r\\n    const normal = normalizeIndicator(text);\\r\\n    if (expected.some(e => e.text !== normal && containsRun(e.text, normal))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is only part of an indicator. Select every word of the phrase.` };\\r\\n    if (expected.some(e => containsRun(normal, e.text))) return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” includes words that are not part of the indicator. Select only the signal words.` };\\r\\n    return { correct: false, hint: `“${text}” is not an indicator in this rule.` };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (wrongGroup.length) return { correct: false, hint: `Check the group for “${wrongGroup[0]}”. Read the whole rule again.` };\\r\\n  return { correct: true, hint: \'\' };\\r\\n}\\r\\n  click(\'Continue\');\\r\\n  const contraLeft = () => screen.getByRole(\'group\', { name: \'Left side\' });\\r\\n  const contraRight = () => screen.getByRole(\'group\', { name: \'Right side\' });\\r\\n  fireEvent.click(within(contraLeft()).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'I\' }));\\r\\n  fireEvent.click(within(contraRight()).getByRole(\'button\', { name: \'O\' }));\\r\\n  fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText(\'NOT on the left side\'));\\r\\n  fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText(\'NOT on the right side\'));\\r\\n  click(\'Check this step\');\\r\\n  expect(screen.getByText(\'Start with the right side of your original diagram. Fixed:
- Added all **25 missing Revisit Drill 5 span entries**, with coverage checks including both revisits.

- Repaired walkthrough permission/obligation transformations, removed automatic advancement, separated adjacent highlights, replaced duplicated/banned walkthrough examples.
- Corrected copy, accepted `Every` in the agreement rule, and replaced coffee/door stories in reviewed derivatives.
- Corrected **52 singular contrapositive openings** across two passes, plus dangling plural openings and corresponding diagram labels.

Preserved all 133 items, IDs, original source statements, source paths/hashes and numbering. Changes to stories affect only reviewed derivatives; correction notes record them.

Verification: **32 focused tests passed across five files**, then the additional walkthrough token/span regression passed, giving **33 passing tests**. Typecheck is still running in session `13446`; root can collect it. Browser acceptance remains root’s final gate.

Remaining decisions: course sequencing, repeated source questions, choosing letters versus phrase diagrams, beer/alcohol source examples, and the embedded `who` highlighting convention. I did not re-key `who` or replace source banks wholesale. No commit, push or deployment performed.

Leaving out arriving after dark would make a claim about all campers.\', instruction: \'Choose the description that keeps the restriction in each rule.\' },\\r\\n  { title: \'Build the diagram\', purpose: \'If the condition on the left is true, what must follow on the right?\', example: \'If a parcel has a blue sticker, it has been inspected.\', explanation: \'A blue sticker is enough to prove inspection. Inspection is required for a blue-sticker parcel. An inspected parcel could still have no blue sticker.\', instruction: \'Choose a side of the arrow, then choose the condition that belongs there. Read the diagram in English before checking.\' },\\r\\n  { title: \'Translate the rule yourself\', purpose: \'Write your own diagram before looking at supplied conditions.\', example: \'Only members use the studio.\', explanation: \'First decide what the rule says you need in order to use the studio. Write both conditions and an arrow, then read your diagram as an if/then sentence.\', instruction: \'Write your diagram and English version. Compare with the choices only after making that attempt.\' },\\r\\n  { title: \'Does the meaning change?\', purpose: \'Different wording can keep a rule or change what it says.\', example: \'Every parcel in Locker 4 has been scanned.\', explanation: \'This tells you something about parcels in Locker 4. Try a possible case that fits one but would break the other.\' },\\r\\n  { title: \'Try unfamiliar cases\', purpose: \'Use the same reasoning with new wording.\', example: \'Visitors must wear a badge.\', explanation: \'This gives visitors an obligation. It does not tell you whether a visitor actually wore a badge. Permission, requirements and completed actions need different labels.\', instruction: \'Read what is stated and choose only what it proves. Keep qualifications and the strength of the claim.\' },\\r\\n  { title: \'Try the distinction in a new case\', purpose: \'Now use the distinctions you revised with different rules.\', example: \'\', explanation: \'The next questions revisit mistakes from this practice with fresh examples.\', instruction: \'Make a new attempt before checking. An answer you reach after feedback is useful practice, but it is different from getting a fresh case right on the first check.\' },\\r\\n];\\r\\n\\r\\ntype DiagramItem = (typeof openingActivityItems)[number];\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The shared refresher items use one-word labels (\\"dancer\\", \\"map\\"). ## assistant

One concrete issue:

**Quiz completion can remain true after content changes.** In `SufficientQuiz.tsx`, a signature mismatch resets the rendered quiz but does not persist that reset. `LessonCompletion` in `OpeningCourse.tsx` reads the old saved `complete: true` directly. A learner who finished the previous quiz but has not marked the lesson read can therefore mark the revised lesson read while its newly reset quiz is unanswered. Persist the invalidation or validate completion against the current signature.

I found no other high-confidence serious regression in hydration gating, sibling lesson-state merging, lesson scoping, Drill 1 result gating or staged/mixed completion. Existing permanent Done status is consistent with the explicitly chosen policy. No files changed during this review.

## assistant

The new opening-course completion records currently **do not reach the legacy learning workflow**.

`LessonCompletion` writes `progress.course[stepId].status = 'done'`. But:

- `recommendNext` in `progress.ts` and `nextSelectedStep` in `routes.tsx` read `progress.lessons[unitId]`.
- Expanded-lesson review checks read edition timestamps in `progress.lessons`.
- The Lessons reviewed total also reads `progress.lessons`.

Therefore, finishing all opening notes can leave the legacy recommendation asking the student to read those units again.

Recommended fix: when recording an explicitly completed **note**, aggregate completion of the notes with its primary `unitId`. Once all required notes for that unit are Done, call `markConditionalLessonRead`, which stamps both the unit and its current edition. Do not stamp on arrival, partial completion or drill completion. Preserve existing dates and student history. `alsoAvailableIn` describes access; it should not automatically confer completion of another unit.

Add tests for partial versus full note completion, edition stamping, reload persistence, and the resulting next-step recommendation. The older route tests at `routes.test.tsx:451` test the explicit legacy “Mark lesson as read” button, so their behavior remains valid; they should not be rewritten into automatic completion. No backend assignment changes are necessary for this local integration fix.

Teamwork alone does not guarantee success.')\n   x['correctionNotes'].append('Preserve the completed occurrence of teamwork stated in the reviewed sentence.')\np.write_text(s[:start]+json.dumps(data,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2)+s[start+end:])\np=Path('src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/SourceWorksheetDrill.tsx');s=p.read_text();needle='''      const a = restored.answers[item.id];\n      if (a &&''';replacement='''      const a = restored.answers[item.id];\n      if (a) {\n        const checked = checkConditionSpans(item.statement, a.spans);\n        if (checked.correct) {\n          // Highlights omit signal words such as must. Symbols keep their semantic force.\n          a.conditionOne = checked.roles[0] === 'sufficient' ? item.sufficient : item.necessary;\n          a.conditionTwo = checked.roles[1] === 'sufficient' ? item.sufficient : item.necessary;\n        }\n      }\n      if (a &&''';assert needle in s;s=s.replace(needle,replacement)\nneedle=\"function advance() { update({ stage: stageIndex + 1, passed: [...new Set([...answer.passed, stage])], revealed: false }); setChecked(false); }\"\nreplacement=\"\"\"function advance() {\n    const meanings = stage === 'conditions' && spanCheck.correct ? {\n      conditionOne: spanCheck.roles[0] === 'sufficient' ? item.sufficient : item.necessary,\n      conditionTwo: spanCheck.roles[1] === 'sufficient' ? item.sufficient : item.necessary,\n    } : {};\n    update({ ...meanings, stage: stageIndex + 1, passed: [...new Set([...answer.passed, stage])], revealed: false });\n    setChecked(false);\n  }\"\"\";assert needle in s;s=s.replace(needle,replacement);p.write_text(s)\np=Path('src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/sourceWorksheetDrills.test.ts');s=p.read_text();s+='''\nit('keeps documentation as a duty and teamwork as an earlier occurrence', () => {\n  const items = sourceWorksheetDrills.flatMap(set => set.items);\n  for (const id of ['source-indicators-11', 'source-roles-10']) {\n    const item = items.find(item => item.id === id)!;\n    expect(item.necessary).toBe('errors are required to be documented before troubleshooting');\n    expect(item.ifThen).toContain('are required to be documented');\n    expect(item.contrapositiveWords).toContain('are not required');\n    expect(item.originalStatement).toBe('Always document errors before troubleshooting.');\n  }\n  const teamwork = items.find(item => item.id === 'source-rephrase-07')!;\n  expect(teamwork.necessary).toContain('has taken place');\n  expect(teamwork.ifThen).toContain('has taken place');\n  expect(teamwork.contrapositiveWords).toContain('has not taken place');\n});\n''';p.write_text(s)\np=Path('src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/SourceWorksheetDrill.test.tsx');s=p.read_text();s+='''\nit('keeps a duty in the symbols even when the highlight leaves must out', () => {\n  run('source-intro-diagrams');\n  fireEvent.change(screen.getByRole('combobox', { name: 'Go to question' }), {target: {value: '3'}});\n  mark(1, 1); mark(3, 5);\n  click('Check this step'); click('Continue');\n  expect(screen.getByText('that person is required to undergo annual training', {selector: 'strong'})).toBeVisible();\n  write('First condition’s symbol', 'M'); write('Second condition’s symbol', 'T');\n  click('Save and continue');\n  expect(screen.getByText(/= /)).toBeDefined();\n  const saved = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('conditional-worksheet-v1:source-intro-diagrams')!);\n  expect(saved.answers['source-intro-diagrams-04'].conditionTwo).toBe('that person is required to undergo annual training');\n  expect(saved.answers['source-intro-diagrams-04'].spans).toEqual([[1, 1], [3, 5]]);\n  cleanup();\n  saved.answers['source-intro-diagrams-04'].conditionTwo = 'undergo annual training';\n  localStorage.setItem('conditional-worksheet-v1:source-intro-diagrams', JSON.stringify(saved));\n  run('source-intro-diagrams');\n  expect(screen.getByText(/that person is required to undergo annual training/)).toBeVisible();\n});\n''';s=s.replace(\"  expect(screen.getByText(/= /)).toBeDefined();\\n\",'');p.write_text(s)\np=Path('output/playwright/conditional-takeover/worksheet-live.js');s=p.read_text(encoding='utf-8').replace(\".getByLabel('Go to question',{exact:true})\", \".getByRole('combobox',{name:'Go to question',exact:true})\")\n# Refresh only fields changed by the reviewed derivative corrections.\na=s.index('const BANK = ')+len('const BANK = ');b=s.index(';\\n',a);bank=json.loads(s[a:b]);allitems={x['id']:x for g in data for x in g['items']}\nfor g in bank.values():\n for item in g['items']:\n  actual=allitems[item['id']]\n  for k in ['statement','sufficient','necessary','ifThen','contrapositiveWords']:item[k]=actual[k]\ns=s[:a]+json.dumps(bank,ensure_ascii=False,separators=(',',':'))+s[b:];p.write_text(s,encoding='utf-8')\n'@ | python -","workdir":"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os-projects\\lsatu-conditional-takeover","max_output_tokens":1000}));
